Skip phases only when explicitly justified.\r\n\r\nWhen exploring the codebase, read `CONTEXT.md` (if it exists) to get a clear mental model of the relevant modules, and check ADRs in the area you're touching.\r\n\r\n## Redact\r\n\r\nThis skill has you show commands, outputs and captured artifacts. **Redact every secret first**: write `<REDACTED>` in its place. If you have a **tight** pass/fail signal for the bug (one that goes red on _this_ bug), you will find the cause; bisection, hypothesis-testing, and instrumentation all just consume it. If you don't have one, no amount of staring at code will save you.\r\n\r\nSpend disproportionate effort here. **Be aggressive. Be creative. Refuse to give up.**\r\n\r\n### Ways to construct one, in roughly this order\r\n\r\n1. **Failing test** at whatever seam reaches the bug: unit, integration, e2e.\r\n2. **Curl / HTTP script** against a running dev server.\r\n3. **CLI invocation** with a fixture input, diffing stdout against a known-good snapshot.\r\n4. **Headless browser script** (Playwright / Puppeteer) that drives the UI and asserts on DOM/console/network.\r\n5. **Replay a captured trace.** Save a real network request / payload / event log to disk; replay it through the code path in isolation.\r\n6. **Throwaway harness.** Spin up a minimal subset of the system (one service, mocked deps) that exercises the bug code path with a single function call.\r\n7. **Property / fuzz loop.** If the bug is \"sometimes wrong output\", run 1000 random inputs and look for the failure mode.\r\n8. **Bisection harness.** If the bug appeared between two known states (commit, dataset, version), automate \"boot at state X, check, repeat\" so you can `git bisect run` it.\r\n9. **Differential loop.** Run the same input through old-version vs new-version (or two configs) and diff outputs.\r\n10. **HITL bash script.** Last resort. If a human must click, drive _them_ with `scripts/hitl-loop.template.sh` so the loop is still structured. Captured output feeds back to you.\r\n\r\nBuild the right feedback loop, and the bug is 90% fixed.\r\n\r\n### Tighten the loop\r\n\r\nTreat the loop as a product. Once you have _a_ loop, **tighten** it:\r\n\r\n- Can I make it faster? (Cache setup, skip unrelated init, narrow the test scope.)\r\n- Can I make the signal sharper? (Assert on the specific symptom, not \"didn't crash\".)\r\n- Can I make it more deterministic? (Pin time, seed RNG, isolate filesystem, freeze network.)\r\n\r\nA 30-second flaky loop is barely better than no loop; a 2-second deterministic one is tight, a debugging superpower.\r\n\r\n### Non-deterministic bugs\r\n\r\nThe goal is not a clean repro but a **higher reproduction rate**. Loop the trigger 100Ã—, parallelise, add stress, narrow timing windows, inject sleeps. A 50%-flake bug is debuggable; 1% is not, so keep raising the rate until it's debuggable.\r\n\r\n### When you genuinely cannot build a loop\r\n\r\nStop and say so explicitly. List what you tried. Ask the user for: (a) access to whatever environment reproduces it, (b) a redacted captured artifact (HAR file, log dump, core dump, screen recording with timestamps), or (c) permission to add temporary production instrumentation. Do **not** proceed to hypothesise without a loop.\r\n\r\n### Completion criterion: a tight loop that goes red\r\n\r\nPhase 1 is done when the loop is **tight** and **red-capable**: you can name **one command** (a script path, a test invocation, a curl) that you have **already run at least once** (show the invocation and its output, redacted), and that is:\r\n\r\n- [ ] **Red-capable**: it drives the actual bug code path and asserts the **user's exact symptom**, so it can go red on this bug and green once fixed. Not \"runs without erroring\"; it must be able to _catch this specific bug_.\r\n- [ ] **Deterministic**: same verdict every run (flaky bugs: a pinned, high reproduction rate, per above).\r\n- [ ] **Fast**: seconds, not minutes.\r\n- [ ] **Agent-runnable**: you can run it unattended; a human in the loop only via `scripts/hitl-loop.template.sh`.\r\n\r\nIf you catch yourself reading code to build a theory before this command exists, **stop: jumping straight to a hypothesis is the exact failure this skill prevents.** No red-capable command, no Phase 2.\r\n\r\n## Phase 2: Reproduce + minimise\r\n\r\nRun the loop. Watch it go red as the bug appears.\r\n\r\nConfirm:\r\n\r\n- [ ] The loop produces the failure mode the **user** described, not a different failure that happens to be nearby. Wrong bug = wrong fix.\r\n- [ ] The failure is reproducible across multiple runs (or, for non-deterministic bugs, reproducible at a high enough rate to debug against).\r\n- [ ] You have captured the exact symptom (error message, wrong output, slow timing) so later phases can verify the fix actually addresses it.\r\n\r\n### Minimise\r\n\r\nOnce it's red, shrink the repro to the **smallest scenario that still goes red**. Cut inputs, callers, config, data, and steps **one at a time**, re-running the loop after each cut, and keep only what's load-bearing for the failure.\r\n\r\nWhy bother: a minimal repro shrinks the hypothesis space in Phase 3 (fewer moving parts left to suspect) and becomes the clean regression test in Phase 5.\r\n\r\nDone when **every remaining element is load-bearing**: removing any one of them makes the loop go green.\r\n\r\nDo not proceed until you have reproduced **and** minimised.\r\n\r\n## Phase 3: Hypothesise\r\n\r\nGenerate **3â€“5 ranked hypotheses** before testing any of them. Single-hypothesis generation anchors on the first plausible idea.\r\n\r\nEach hypothesis must be **falsifiable**: state the prediction it makes.\r\n\r\n> Format: \"If <X> is the cause, then <changing Y> will make the bug disappear / <changing Z> will make it worse.\"\r\n\r\nIf you cannot state the prediction, the hypothesis is a vibe: discard or sharpen it.\r\n\r\n**Show the ranked list to the user before testing.** They often have domain knowledge that re-ranks instantly (\"we just deployed a change to #3\"), or know hypotheses they've already ruled out. Cheap checkpoint, big time saver. Don't block on it; proceed with your ranking if the user is AFK.\r\n\r\n## Phase 4: Instrument\r\n\r\nEach probe must map to a specific prediction from Phase 3. **Change one variable at a time.**\r\n\r\nTool preference:\r\n\r\n1. **Debugger / REPL inspection** if the env supports it. One breakpoint beats ten logs.\r\n2. **Targeted logs** at the boundaries that distinguish hypotheses.\r\n3. Never \"log everything and grep\".\r\n\r\n**Tag every debug log** with a unique prefix, e.g. `[DEBUG-a4f2]`. Cleanup at the end becomes a single grep. Untagged logs survive; tagged logs die.\r\n\r\n**Perf branch.** For performance regressions, logs are usually wrong. Instead: establish a baseline measurement (timing harness, `performance.now()`, profiler, query plan), then bisect. Measure first, fix second.\r\n\r\n## Phase 5: Fix + regression test\r\n\r\nWrite the regression test **before the fix**, but only if there is a **correct seam** for it.\r\n\r\nA correct seam is one where the test exercises the **real bug pattern** as it occurs at the call site. If the only available seam is too shallow (single-caller test when the bug needs multiple callers, unit test that can't replicate the chain that triggered the bug), a regression test there gives false confidence.\r\n\r\n**If no correct seam exists, that itself is the finding.** Note it. The codebase architecture is preventing the bug from being locked down. Flag this for the next phase.\r\n\r\nIf a correct seam exists:\r\n\r\n1. Turn the minimised repro into a failing test at that seam.\r\n2. Watch it fail.\r\n3. Apply the fix.\r\n4. Watch it pass.\r\n5. Re-run the Phase 1 feedback loop against the original (un-minimised) scenario.\r\n\r\n## Phase 6: Cleanup\r\n\r\nRequired before declaring done:\r\n\r\n- [ ] Original repro no longer reproduces (re-run the Phase 1 loop)\r\n- [ ] Regression test passes (or absence of seam is documented)\r\n- [ ] All `[DEBUG-...]` instrumentation removed (`grep` the prefix)\r\n- [ ] Throwaway prototypes deleted (or moved to a clearly-marked debug location)\r\n- [ ] The hypothesis that turned out correct is stated in the commit / PR message, so the next debugger learns\r\n"}}

Inspect the existing implementation before making changes.\r\n2. Understand the relevant modules and their dependencies.\r\n3. Read the applicable coding standard:\r\n   - Frontend: `docs/frontend/coding.standard.md`\r\n   - Backend: `docs/backend/coding.standard.md`\r\n4. Check related tests and existing patterns.\r\n5. Prefer extending existing abstractions over introducing new ones.\r\n\r\n## Architecture Rules\r\n\r\n- Preserve the Modular Monolith architecture.\r\n- Keep module boundaries explicit.\r\n- Keep business logic independent of framework-specific concerns.\r\n- Do not introduce unnecessary abstractions or dependencies.\r\n- Avoid unrelated refactoring.\r\n- Do not duplicate existing functionality.\r\n\r\n## Implementation Rules\r\n\r\n- Follow the relevant coding standard.\r\n- Use strict TypeScript types. Avoid `any`.\r\n- Handle errors and asynchronous operations explicitly.\r\n- Validate external input.\r\n- Consider transaction boundaries and data consistency.\r\n- Add tests for new or changed behavior.\r\n- Never silently change existing API contracts.\r\n\r\n## Before Completing a Task\r\n\r\nRun applicable checks:\r\n\r\n1. Lint.\r\n2. Type checking.\r\n3. Relevant unit and integration tests.\r\n4. Build.\r\n\r\nIf a check cannot run, explain why.\r\n\r\n## Final Response\r\n\r\nSummarize:\r\n\r\n- What changed.\r\n- Important implementation decisions.\r\n- Tests and validation performed.\r\n- Known limitations or remaining work.\r\n\r\nDo not claim that a test, build, or validation passed unless it was actually executed.\r\n\r\n\r\n## Feature Development Workflow\r\n\r\nFor behavior-changing features, enhancements, significant refactors, and bug fixes, follow `docs/workflow-coding.md`. It defines the grilling confirmation gate, BDD scenario traceability, scaled feature artifacts, and the TDD cycle.\r\n# Feature Development Workflow\r\n\r\nUse this workflow for features, enhancements, significant refactors, and bug fixes that change behavior. Scale the documentation to the change, but keep the clarification, BDD, and TDD gates for behavior changes.\r\n\r\n## 1. Understand and clarify\r\n\r\nInspect the existing implementation, related tests, architecture, project context, applicable coding standards, and available test commands. Establish repository facts yourself before asking the user.\r\n\r\nFor behavior-changing work, invoke the `grilling` skill:\r\n\r\n- Start with a short clarification round for a straightforward change. Expand into a full design-tree interview when decisions are ambiguous, risky, or have competing solutions.\r\n- Ask all currently answerable questions in a round, number them, and give a recommended answer for each. Wait for the user's answers, then recompute the next question frontier.\r\n- Do not ask the user to discover facts available in the repository or environment.\r\n- When the frontier is settled, present a concise shared-understanding summary covering requirements, constraints, scenarios, design choices, and material test seams. Wait for explicit user confirmation before treating the specification as approved or implementing. If the user declines or asks to stop, stop.\r\n\r\n## 2. Specify and write BDD scenarios\r\n\r\nStore approved feature artifacts under `specs/<feature-name>/`. `.scratch/` may hold temporary discovery notes and issue-tracking tickets; promote the approved specification into `specs/` before implementation.\r\n\r\nFor a feature, enhancement, or significant change, create or update:\r\n\r\n- `spec.md`: requirements, constraints, acceptance criteria, and stable-ID Given/When/Then scenarios.\r\n- `design.md`: architecture, components, API contracts, data model, and material test seams.\r\n- `plan.md`: implementation approach and milestones.\r\n- `task.md`: ordered tasks and validation evidence.\r\n\r\nFor a narrow bug fix, use a concise `spec.md` for the expected behavior and relevant scenarios, plus `task.md` for implementation and test evidence. Add `design.md` or `plan.md` when the fix has material design or sequencing decisions. Expand the artifacts if the scope grows.\r\n\r\nTrace every acceptance criterion to one or more BDD scenarios. Include meaningful failure and boundary cases; do not create a separate scenario for every trivial variant. BDD scenarios describe observable behavior and are the source for implementation-level test coverage.\r\n\r\n## 3. Review before implementation\r\n\r\nConfirm that:\r\n\r\n- The user-approved understanding, requirements, and constraints are reflected in the spec.\r\n- Acceptance criteria and BDD scenarios are clear, observable, and traceable.\r\n- The design, public test seams, and test layers fit the architecture and have no unresolved material decisions.\r\n- Tasks are actionable and small enough for vertical implementation slices.\r\n\r\nResolve gaps with the user before implementation. Do not implement behavior outside the approved specification.\r\n\r\n## 4. Implement BDD-first with TDD\r\n\r\nUse BDD to define behavior, then TDD to implement it at the most appropriate test layer. Trace each scenario to automated verification, choosing the lowest practical layer that proves the behavior. Reserve end-to-end tests for journeys that need cross-boundary validation; a scenario does not require a one-to-one end-to-end test.\r\n\r\nWork in vertical slices, one behavior at a time:\r\n\r\n1. **Red:** Add a meaningful test for an approved behavior and run it. Confirm it fails because the behavior is missing or incorrect, not because of setup or environment failure.\r\n2. **Green:** Make the smallest production change that passes the test and relevant existing tests.\r\n3. **Refactor:** Improve the code while keeping the tests green.\r\n\r\nRecord the test layer, command, expected Red result, and Green result in `task.md`. If the Red test cannot be run, record the blocker and do not claim that the TDD cycle is complete. For non-behavioral edits, a new failing test is unnecessary; mechanical refactors still need characterization coverage for the behavior they preserve.\r\n\r\nFollow the applicable coding standards:\r\n\r\n- `docs/backend/coding.standard.md`\r\n- `docs/frontend/coding.standard.md`\r\n\r\n## 5. Verify and complete\r\n\r\n- Run relevant tests, lint, type-check, and build commands for the affected projects.\r\n- Verify each acceptance criterion against its BDD scenarios and test evidence.\r\n- Check for regressions and unintended behavior changes.\r\n- Keep the implementation, specification, design, plan, and task evidence synchronized.\r\n- Mark tasks complete only when their checks have actually run and passed.\r\n\r\nReport what changed, important decisions, actual validation results, and remaining limitations. Never claim a test or validation passed unless it was executed.\r\n"}}

The codebase architecture is preventing the bug from being locked down. Flag this for the next phase.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf a correct seam exists:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Turn the minimised repro into a failing test at that seam.\\r\\n2. Watch it fail.\\r\\n3. Apply the fix.\\r\\n4. Watch it pass.\\r\\n5. Re-run the Phase 1 feedback loop against the original (un-minimised) scenario.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Phase 6: Cleanup\\r\\n\\r\\nRequired before declaring done:\\r\\n\\r\\n- [ ] Original repro no longer reproduces (re-run the Phase 1 loop)\\r\\n- [ ] Regression test passes (or absence of seam is documented)\\r\\n- [ ] All `[DEBUG-...]` instrumentation removed (`grep` the prefix)\\r\\n- [ ] Throwaway prototypes deleted (or moved to a clearly-marked debug location)\\r\\n- [ ] The hypothesis that turned out correct is stated in the commit / PR message, so the next debugger learns\\r\\n\"}}"

Implement BDD-first with TDD\\r\\n\\r\\nUse BDD to define behavior, then TDD to implement it at the most appropriate test layer. Trace each scenario to automated verification, choosing the lowest practical layer that proves the behavior. Reserve end-to-end tests for journeys that need cross-boundary validation; a scenario does not require a one-to-one end-to-end test.\\r\\n\\r\\nWork in vertical slices, one behavior at a time:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Red:** Add a meaningful test for an approved behavior and run it. Confirm it fails because the behavior is missing or incorrect, not because of setup or environment failure.\\r\\n2. **Green:** Make the smallest production change that passes the test and relevant existing tests.\\r\\n3. **Refactor:** Improve the code while keeping the tests green.\\r\\n\\r\\nRecord the test layer, command, expected Red result, and Green result in `task.md`. {"index":0,"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"943c4c","wall_time_seconds":0.0000448,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3995,"output":"# Lifecycle Hooks (`hooks.json`)\r\n\r\nLifecycle hooks allow you to execute external shell commands or scripts at\r\nspecific points during the Agent's execution loop. This is powerful for\r\nenforcing safety guards, running linters, auto-formatting code, or capturing\r\ncustom diagnostics.\r\n\r\nHooks are configured in a single `hooks.json` file placed in your customization\r\nroot directory (e.g., `.agents/hooks.json`).\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## File Format\r\n\r\nThe `hooks.json` file is a JSON object where each top-level key is a **hook\r\nname**, mapping to its event configuration.\r\n\r\n```json\r\n{\r\n  \"lint-checker\": {\r\n    \"PostToolUse\": [\r\n      {\r\n        \"matcher\": \"run_command\",\r\n        \"hooks\": [\r\n          {\r\n            \"type\": \"command\",\r\n            \"command\": \"./scripts/lint.sh\",\r\n            \"timeout\": 10\r\n          }\r\n        ]\r\n      }\r\n    ]\r\n  },\r\n  \"safety-gate\": {\r\n    \"enabled\": false,\r\n    \"PreToolUse\": [\r\n      {\r\n        \"matcher\": \"run_command\",\r\n        \"hooks\": [\r\n          {\r\n            \"command\": \"./scripts/safety-check.sh\"\r\n          }\r\n        ]\r\n      }\r\n    ]\r\n  },\r\n  \"reminder\": {\r\n    \"PreInvocation\": [\r\n      {\r\n        \"type\": \"command\",\r\n        \"command\": \"./scripts/reminder.sh\"\r\n      }\r\n    ]\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n*   **Merging**: Multiple named hooks (e.g., from different plugins or configs)\r\n    for the same event type are merged and executed sequentially.\r\n*   **Disabling**: Set `\"enabled\": false` at the hook level to temporarily\r\n    disable all its handlers.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Hook Spec Fields\r\n\r\nEach named hook supports:\r\n\r\n*   **`enabled`** (bool, optional): Defaults to `true`. Set to `false` to\r\n    disable.\r\n*   **`SessionStart`** (array, optional): Handlers running once when a new\r\n    conversation session starts.\r\n*   **`PreToolUse`** (array, optional): Handlers running before a tool executes.\r\n*   **`PostToolUse`** (array, optional): Handlers running after a tool\r\n    completes.\r\n*   **`PreInvocation`** (array, optional): Handlers running before the model is\r\n    called.\r\n*   **`PostInvocation`** (array, optional): Handlers running after tool calls\r\n    finish.\r\n*   **`Stop`** (array, optional): Handlers running when the execution directly).        :\r\n\r\n### The Matcher\r\n\r\nFor tool-specific events (`PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`), you must wrap the\r\nhandlers in a group with a `matcher` regex:\r\n\r\n*   `\"matcher\": \"*\"` or `\"\"`: Matches all tools.\r\n*   `\"matcher\": \"run_command\"`: Matches exactly `run_command`.\r\n*   `\"matcher\": \"run_command\\|view_file\"`: Matches either tool.\r\n*   `\"matcher\": \"browser_.*\"`: Matches any tool starting with `browser_`.\r\n\r\nTool names are derived by lowercasing the step type and removing the\r\n`CORTEX_STEP_TYPE_` prefix.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Hook Handler Fields\r\n\r\nEach individual hook handler object supports:\r\n\r\n*   **`type`** (string, optional): Defaults to `\"command\"`. Only `\"command\"`\r\n    (shell execution) is currently supported.\r\n*   **`command`** (string, required): The shell command to execute (run via `sh\r\n    -c` on Unix, `cmd /c` on Windows). `~` is expanded to the home directory.\r\n    The working directory is set to the directory containing `hooks.json`.\r\n*   **`timeout`** (int, optional): Execution timeout in seconds. Defaults to\r\n    `30`.\r\n\r\nCommand hooks that ship with a plugin also get `PLUGIN_ROOT` (the plugin\r\ndirectory) and, for installed plugins, `PLUGIN_DATA` (the plugin's persistent\r\ndata directory) as environment variables, the same paths the plugin's MCP\r\nservers get. They are not substituted into `command`: hooks run in the plugin\r\nfolder, so refer to bundled files with a relative path, and use the variables\r\nfor the rest, e.g. `\"command\": \"./hooks/check.sh --cache \\\"$PLUGIN_DATA\\\"\"`.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Input/Output Contract\r\n\r\nHook commands receive context as a JSON object on **stdin** and must output\r\ntheir result as a JSON object on **stdout**.\r\n\r\n> [!IMPORTANT] All JSON keys in the hook payloads use **camelCase** (protojson\r\n> encoding), e.g., `conversationId` and `stepIdx`.\r\n\r\n### Common Input Fields\r\n\r\nEvery hook payload sent to `stdin` includes these common system metadata fields:\r\n\r\n```json\r\n{\r\n  \"conversationId\": \"ec33ebf9-0cba-4100-8142-c61503f6c587\",\r\n  \"workspacePaths\": [\"/path/to/workspace\"],\r\n  \"transcriptPath\": \"/path/to/workspace/.gemini/antigravity/transcript.jsonl\",\r\n  \"artifactDirectoryPath\": \"/path/to/workspace/.gemini/antigravity/artifacts\",\r\n  \"modelName\": \"auto\",\r\n  \"lastUserInput\": \"Fix the failing unit test\",\r\n  \"hookEventName\": \"PreToolUse\",\r\n  \"cwd\": \"/path/to/workspace\"\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n`hookEventName` is the event being run, as written in `hooks.json`. `cwd` is the\r\nagent's working directory, where its commands run by default; it is not the hook\r\nprocess's working directory.\r\n\r\n> [!NOTE]\r\n> The `transcriptPath` and `artifactDirectoryPath` are product-specific. The\r\n> example above uses `antigravity` (for Antigravity 2.0). Depending on the\r\n> interface you are using, the directory name will differ:\r\n> *   **CLI**: `antigravity-cli/`\r\n> *   **Antigravity 2.0**: `antigravity/`\r\n> *   **IDE**: `antigravity-ide/`\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 1. `PreToolUse` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to gate, block, or audit tool executions.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"toolCall\": {\r\n        \"id\": \"toolu_01\",\r\n        \"name\": \"run_command\",\r\n        \"args\": {\r\n          \"CommandLine\": \"npm test\"\r\n        }\r\n      },\r\n      \"stepIdx\": 19,\r\n      ... (common fields)\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"decision\": \"ask\",\r\n      \"reason\": \"Requires confirmation for test execution.\",\r\n      \"permissionOverrides\": [\"command(npm test)\"]\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`decision`** (string, required):\r\n        *   `\"allow\"`: Automatically allow the tool execution.\r\n        *   `\"deny\"`: Hard block the execution immediately.\r\n        *   `\"ask\"`: Prompt the user for permission (respects \"Always Allow\"\r\n            cache).\r\n        *   `\"force_ask\"`: Always prompt the user, ignoring cached permissions.\r\n    *   **`reason`** (string, optional): Explanation shown to the user/agent.\r\n    *   **`permissionOverrides`** (array of strings, optional): Temporary\r\n        permission grants.\r\n    *   **`overwrite`** (object, optional): Key-value pairs merged into the tool\r\n        call's arguments before it runs. This is a **shallow, top-level** merge:\r\n        each key replaces the value at that key in the tool call's arguments\r\n        (nested objects are replaced wholesale, not deep-merged). The modified\r\n        tool call is what actually executes and is recorded. Example:\r\n\r\n        ```json\r\n        {\r\n          \"decision\": \"allow\",\r\n          \"overwrite\": {\r\n            \"CommandLine\": \"ls -la\"\r\n          }\r\n        }\r\n        ```\r\n\r\n        The agent replays the tool call it originally emitted, so when\r\n        `overwrite` changes the arguments the tool result is prefixed with a\r\n        notice telling the agent that a hook rewrote them, naming the keys that\r\n        changed.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 2. `PostToolUse` Contract\r\n\r\nUse for post-execution cleanup, auto-fixes, or analysis.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"toolCall\": {\r\n        \"id\": \"toolu_01\", // Same id as in PreToolUse for this call\r\n        \"name\": \"run_command\",\r\n        \"args\": {\r\n          \"CommandLine\": \"npm test\"\r\n        }\r\n      },\r\n      \"stepIdx\": 5,\r\n      \"error\": \"exit status 1\", // Present if the tool failed\r\n      ... (common fields)\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**: Expects an empty JSON object `{}`.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 3. `SessionStart` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to inject welcome messages, initial instructions, or startup context once at\r\nthe beginning of a new conversation session.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**: Common system metadata fields (`conversationId`,\r\n    `workspacePaths`, `transcriptPath`, `artifactDirectoryPath`, `modelName`).\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"injectSteps\": [\r\n        {\r\n          \"systemMessage\": {\r\n            \"systemMessage\": \"Welcome! Custom workspace environment is active.\"\r\n          }\r\n        }\r\n      ]\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array of objects, optional): Steps to inject at\r\n        session startup. Accepts the same step types as `PreInvocation` (see\r\n        below).\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 4. `PreInvocation` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to gate user prompts or inject context or instructions before the model\r\nruns.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"invocationNum\": 3,\r\n      \"initialNumSteps\": 10,\r\n      ... (common fields)\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"injectSteps\": [\r\n        {\r\n          \"systemMessage\": {\r\n            \"systemMessage\": \"Remember to check for lint errors before proposing changes.\"\r\n          }\r\n        }\r\n      ],\r\n      \"terminationBehavior\": \"\"\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array of objects, optional): Steps to inject.\r\n        Supported step types:\r\n        *   `{\"systemMessage\": {\"systemMessage\": \"...\", \"metadata\": {...}}}`\r\n            (persists in the conversation trajectory across turns; note that\r\n            `systemMessage` requires a nested object rather than a plain string).\r\n            To show markdown to the user that the model never reads, set\r\n            `metadata.user_markdown`: the UI renders it inline instead of a\r\n            collapsible \"System Message\" row, and the model sees only the\r\n            `systemMessage` text (leave it `\"\"` to send the model nothing).\r\n        *   `{\"userMessage\": \"...\"}` (plain string)\r\n        *   `{\"ephemeralMessage\": \"...\"}` *(deprecated — use `systemMessage`\r\n            instead; converted to a hidden system message)*\r\n        *   `{\"errorMessage\": {\"userMessage\": \"...\"}}` (shows an error message\r\n            to the user without sending it to the model. Marks the turn as\r\n            errored with a non-zero exit code in non-interactive `-p` mode, and\r\n            does not stop execution on its own unless paired with\r\n            `\"terminationBehavior\": \"terminate\"`).\r\n    *   **`terminationBehavior`** (string, optional):\r\n        *   `\"terminate\"`: Stops execution immediately before calling the model\r\n            (pair with an injected `errorMessage` to show a block message to the\r\n            user).\r\n        *   `\"\"` (or omitted): Proceeds to the model call (default).\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 5. `PostInvocation` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to inspect model outputs and potentially force continuation.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**: Same as `PreInvocation` input.\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"injectSteps\": [],\r\n      \"terminationBehavior\": \"force_continue\"\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array, optional): Steps to inject (same supported\r\n        step types as `PreInvocation`).\r\n    *   **`terminationBehavior`** (string, optional):\r\n        *   `\"force_continue\"`: Forces the execution loop to continue.\r\n        *   `\"terminate\"`: Forces the loop to stop (pair with an injected\r\n            `errorMessage` to show a block message to the user).\r\n        *   `\"\"` (or omitted): Default behavior.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 6. `Stop` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to prevent the agent from stopping if goals are not met.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"executionNum\": 1,\r\n      \"terminationReason\": \"NO_TOOL_CALL\", // e.g., \"NO_TOOL_CALL\", \"MAX_INVOCATIONS\", \"ERROR\", \"USER_CANCELED\", \"TERMINAL_STEP_TYPE\"\r\n      \"error\": \"\", // Present if stopped due to error\r\n      \"fullyIdle\": true, // true if all background tasks are done\r\n      \"continuationActive\": true, // true after a Stop hook kept the agent going this turn (executionNum > 0)\r\n      ... (common fields)\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n    *   **`continuationActive`** (boolean): `true` while the agent is doing\r\n        work that a `Stop` hook asked for in this turn; `false` again once a\r\n        new user message starts a turn. Check it so a hook that asks the agent\r\n        to continue doesn't ask again in a loop.\r\n    *   **`terminationReason`** (string): The `ExecutorTerminationReason` enum\r\n        name with the `EXECUTOR_TERMINATION_REASON_` prefix stripped:\r\n        *   `\"NO_TOOL_CALL\"`: Normal turn completion (model finished without\r\n            calling a tool).\r\n        *   `\"MAX_INVOCATIONS\"`: Hit the maximum invocation limit.\r\n        *   `\"MAX_FORCED_INVOCATIONS\"`: Hit the forced-continuation limit.\r\n        *   `\"MAX_TOKEN_BUDGET_EXCEEDED\"`: Exceeded the token budget.\r\n        *   `\"ERROR\"`: Execution terminated due to an error.\r\n        *   `\"USER_CANCELED\"`: Canceled by the user.\r\n        *   `\"TERMINAL_STEP_TYPE\"`: Stopped on a terminal step type.\r\n        *   `\"TERMINAL_CUSTOM_HOOK\"`: Terminated by a custom `PreInvocation` or\r\n            `PostInvocation` hook.\r\n        *   `\"INJECTED_RESPONSE\"`: Terminated after an injected response.\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"decision\": \"continue\",\r\n      \"reason\": \"The tests are still running in the background. Please wait.\"\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`decision`** (string, required): Set to `\"continue\"` to block the stop\r\n        and re-enter the loop. Any other value allows the agent to stop.\r\n    *   **`reason`** (string, optional): Injected as a system message if\r\n        continuing.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Current Limitations\r\n\r\n*   Only `type: \"command\"` is supported (no HTTP or prompt hooks yet).\r\n*   Hooks run synchronously and block the agent loop (no async execution).\r\n"}}

      "text": "Warning: truncated output (original token count: 10698)\nTotal output lines: 3\n\n{\"index\":0,\"status\":\"fulfilled\",\"value\":{\"chunk_id\":\"943c4c\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.0000448,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":3995,\"output\":\"# Lifecycle Hooks (`hooks.json`)\\r\\n\\r\\nLifecycle hooks allow you to execute external shell commands or scripts at\\r\\nspecific points during the Agent's execution loop. This is powerful for\\r\\nenforcing safety guards, running linters, auto-formatting code, or capturing\\r\\ncustom diagnostics.\\r\\n\\r\\nHooks are configured in a single `hooks.json` file placed in your customization\\r\\nroot directory (e.g., `.agents/hooks.json`).\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n## File Format\\r\\n\\r\\nThe `hooks.json` file is a JSON object where each top-level key is a **hook\\r\\nname**, mapping to its event configuration.\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\\"lint-checker\\\": {\\r\\n    \\\"PostToolUse\\\": [\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"matcher\\\": \\\"run_command\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"hooks\\\": [\\r\\n          {\\r\\n            \\\"type\\\": \\\"command\\\",\\r\\n            \\\"command\\\": \\\"./scripts/lint.sh\\\",\\r\\n            \\\"timeout\\\": 10\\r\\n          }\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    ]\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  \\\"safety-gate\\\": {\\r\\n    \\\"enabled\\\": false,\\r\\n    \\\"PreToolUse\\\": [\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"matcher\\\": \\\"run_command\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"hooks\\\": [\\r\\n          {\\r\\n            \\\"command\\\": \\\"./scripts/safety-check.sh\\\"\\r\\n          }\\r\\n        ]\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    ]\\r\\n  },\\r\\n  \\\"reminder\\\": {\\r\\n    \\\"PreInvocation\\\": [\\r\\n      {\\r\\n        \\\"type\\\": \\\"command\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"command\\\": \\\"./scripts/reminder.sh\\\"\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    ]\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Merging**: Multiple named hooks (e.g., from different plugins or configs)\\r\\n    for the same event type are merged and executed sequentially.\\r\\n*   **Disabling**: Set `\\\"enabled\\\": false` at the hook level to temporarily\\r\\n    disable all its handlers.\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n## Hook Spec Fields\\r\\n\\r\\nEach named hook supports:\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **`enabled`** (bool, optional): Defaults to `true`. Set to `false` to\\r\\n    disable.\\r\\n*   **`SessionStart`** (array, optional): Handlers running once when a new\\r\\n    conversation session starts.\\r\\n*   **`PreToolUse`** (array, optional): Handlers running before a tool executes.\\r\\n*   **`PostToolUse`** (array, optional): Handlers running after a tool\\r\\n    completes.\\r\\n*   **`PreInvocation`** (array, optional): Handlers running before the model is\\r\\n    called.\\r\\n*   **`PostInvocation`** (array, optional): Handlers running after tool calls\\r\\n    finish.\\r\\n*   **`Stop`** (array, optional): Handlers running when the execution loop\\r\\n    terminates.\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n## Supported Event Types\\r\\n\\r\\n| Event            | When it fires     | Matcher target   | Structure         |\\r\\n| :--------------- | :---------------- | :--------------- | :---------------- |\\r\\n| `SessionStart`   | Once when a new   | N/A (ignored).   | Flat (list of     |\\r\\n:                  : session starts.   :                  : handler objects   :\\r\\n:                  :                   :                  : directly).        :\\r\\n| `PreToolUse`     | Before a tool     | Tool name (e.g., | Grouped (uses     |\\r\\n:                  : step executes.    : `run_command`).  : `matcher` &       :\\r\\n:                  :                   :                  : `hooks` wrapper). :\\r\\n| `PostToolUse`    | After a tool step | Tool name (e.g., | Grouped (uses     |\\r\\n:                  : completes.        : `run_command`).  : `matcher` &       :\\r\\n:                  :                   :                  : `hooks` wrapper). :\\r\\n| `PreInvocation`  | Before the model  | N/A (ignored).   | Flat (list of     |\\r\\n:                  : is called.        :                  : handler objects   :\\r\\n:                  :                   :                  : directly).        :\\r\\n| `PostInvocation` | After tool calls  | N/A (ignored).   | Flat (list of     |\\r\\n:                  : finish.           :                  : handler objects   :\\r\\n:                  :                   :                  : directly).        :\\r\\n| `Stop`           | When the          | N/A (ignored).   | Flat (list of     |\\r\\n:                  : execution loop    :                  : handler objects   :\\r\\n:                  : terminates.       :                  : directly).        :\\r\\n\\r\\n### The Matcher\\r\\n\\r\\nFor tool-specific events (`PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`), you must wrap the\\r\\nhandlers in a group with a `matcher` regex:\\r\\n\\r\\n*   `\\\"matcher\\\": \\\"*\\\"` or `\\\"\\\"`: Matches all tools.\\r\\n*   `\\\"matcher\\\": \\\"run_command\\\"`: Matches exactly `run_command`.\\r\\n*   `\\\"matcher\\\": \\\"run_command\\\\|view_file\\\"`: Matches either tool.\\r\\n*   `\\\"matcher\\\": \\\"browser_.*\\\"`: Matches any tool starting with `browser_`.\\r\\n\\r\\nTool names are derived by lowercasing the step type and removing the\\r\\n`CORTEX_STEP_TYPE_` prefix.\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n## Hook Handler Fields\\r\\n\\r\\nEach individual hook handler object supports:\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **`type`** (string, optional): Defaults to `\\\"command\\\"`. Only `\\\"command\\\"`\\r\\n    (shell execution) is currently supported.\\r\\n*   **`command`** (string, required): The shell command to execute (run via `sh\\r\\n    -c` on Unix, `cmd /c` on Windows). `~` is expanded to the home directory.\\r\\n    The working directory is set to the directory containing `hooks.json`.\\r\\n*   **`timeout`** (int, optional): Execution timeout in seconds. Defaults to\\r\\n    `30`.\\r\\n\\r\\nCommand hooks that ship with a plugin also get `PLUGIN_ROOT` (the plugin\\r\\ndirectory) and, for installed plugins, `PLUGIN_DATA` (the plugin's persistent\\r\\ndata directory) as environment variables, the same paths the plugin's MCP\\r\\nservers get. They are not substituted into `command`: hooks run in the plugin\\r\\nfolder, so refer to bundled files with a relative path, and use the variables\\r\\nfor the rest, e.g. `\\\"command\\\": \\\"./hooks/check.sh --cache \\\\\\\"$PLUGIN_DATA\\\\\\\"\\\"`.\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n## Input/Output Contract\\r\\n\\r\\nHook commands receive context as a JSON object on **stdin** and must output\\r\\ntheir result as a JSON object on **stdout**.\\r\\n\\r\\n> [!IMPORTANT] All JSON keys in the hook payloads use **camelCase** (protojson\\r\\n> encoding), e.g., `conversationId` and `stepIdx`.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Common Input Fields\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery hook payload sent to `stdin` includes these common system metadata fields:\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\\"conversationId\\\": \\\"ec33ebf9-0cba-4100-8142-c61503f6c587\\\",\\r\\n  \\\"workspacePaths\\\": [\\\"/path/to/workspace\\\"],\\r\\n  \\\"transcriptPath\\\": \\\"/path/to/workspace/.gemini/antigravity/transcript.jsonl\\\",\\r\\n  \\\"artifactDirectoryPath\\\": \\\"/path/to/workspace/.gemini/antigravity/artifacts\\\",\\r\\n  \\\"modelName\\\": \\\"auto\\\",\\r\\n  \\\"lastUserInput\\\": \\\"Fix the failing unit test\\\",\\r\\n  \\\"hookEventName\\\": \\\"PreToolUse\\\",\\r\\n  \\\"cwd\\\": \\\"/path/to/workspace\\\"\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n`hookEventName` is the event being run, as written in `hooks.json`. `cwd` is the\\r\\nagent's working directory, where its commands run by default; it is not the hook\\r\\nprocess's working directory.\\r\\n\\r\\n> [!NOTE]\\r\\n> The `transcriptPath` and `artifactDirectoryPath` are product-specific. The\\r\\n> example above uses `antigravity` (for Antigravity 2.0). Depending on the\\r\\n> interface you are using, the directory name will differ:\\r\\n> *   **CLI**: `antigravity-cli/`\\r\\n> *   **Antigravity 2.0**: `antigravity/`\\r\\n> *   **IDE**: `antigravity-ide/`\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. `PreToolUse` Contract\\r\\n\\r\\nUse to gate, block, or audit tool executions.\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"toolCall\\\": {\\r\\n        \\\"id\\\": \\\"toolu_01\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"name\\\": \\\"run_command\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": {\\r\\n          \\\"CommandLine\\\": \\\"npm test\\\"\\r\\n        }\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      \\\"stepIdx\\\": 19,\\r\\n      ... (common fields)\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"decision\\\": \\\"ask\\\",\\r\\n      \\\"reason\\\": \\\"Requires confirmation for test execution.\\\",\\r\\n      \\\"permissionOverrides\\\": [\\\"command(npm test)\\\"]\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n\\r\\n    *   **`decision`** (string, required):\\r\\n        *   `\\\"allow\\\"`: Automatically allow the tool execution.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"deny\\\"`: Hard block the execution immediately.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"ask\\\"`: Prompt the user for permission (respects \\\"Always Allow\\\"\\r\\n            cache).\\r\\n        *   `\\\"force_ask\\\"`: Always prompt the user, ignoring cached permissions.\\r\\n    *   **`reason`** (string, optional): Explanation shown to the user/agent.\\r\\n    *   **`permissionOverrides`** (array of strings, optional): Temporary\\r\\n        permission grants.\\r\\n    *   **`overwrite`** (object, optional): Key-value pairs merged into the tool\\r\\n        call's arguments before it runs. This is a **shallow, top-level** merge:\\r\\n        each key replaces the value at that key in the tool call's arguments\\r\\n        (nested objects are replaced wholesale, not deep-merged). The modified\\r\\n        tool call is what actually executes and is recorded. Example:\\r\\n\\r\\n        ```json\\r\\n        {\\r\\n          \\\"decision\\\": \\\"allow\\\",\\r\\n          \\\"overwrite\\\": {\\r\\n            \\\"CommandLine\\\": \\\"ls -la\\\"\\r\\n          }\\r\\n        }\\r\\n        ```\\r\\n\\r\\n        The agent replays the tool call it originally emitted, so when\\r\\n        `overwrite` changes the arguments the tool result is prefixed with a\\r\\n        notice telling the agent that a hook rewrote them, naming the keys that\\r\\n        changed.\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. `PostToolUse` Contract\\r\\n\\r\\nUse for post-execution cleanup, auto-fixes, or analysis.\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"toolCall\\\": {\\r\\n        \\\"id\\\": \\\"toolu_01\\\", // Same id as in PreToolUse for this call\\r\\n        \\\"name\\\": \\\"run_command\\\",\\r\\n        \\\"args\\\": {\\r\\n          \\\"CommandLine\\\": \\\"npm test\\\"\\r\\n        }\\r\\n      },\\r\\n      \\\"stepIdx\\\": 5,\\r\\n      \\\"error\\\": \\\"exit status 1\\\", // Present if the tool failed\\r\\n      ... (common fields)\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Output (stdout)**: Expects an empty JSON object `{}`.\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. `SessionStart` Contract\\r\\n\\r\\nUse to inject welcome messages, initial instructions, or startup context once at\\r\\nthe beginning of a new conversation session.\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Input (stdin)**: Common system metadata fields (`conversationId`,\\r\\n    `workspacePaths`, `transcriptPath`, `artifactDirectoryPath`, `modelName`).\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"injectSteps\\\": [\\r\\n        {\\r\\n          \\\"systemMessage\\\": {\\r\\n            \\\"systemMessage\\\": \\\"Welcome! Custom workspace environment is active.\\\"\\r\\n          }\\r\\n        }\\r\\n      ]\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n\\r\\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array of objects, optional): Steps to inject at\\r\\n        session startup. Accepts the same step types as `PreInvocation` (see\\r\\n        below).\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n### 4. `PreInvocation` Contract\\r\\n\\r\\nUse to gate user prompts or inject context or instructions before the model\\r\\nruns.\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"invocationNum\\\": 3,\\r\\n      \\\"initialNumSteps\\\": 10,\\r\\n      ... (common fields)\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"injectSteps\\\": [\\r\\n        {\\r\\n          \\\"systemMessage\\\": {\\r\\n            \\\"systemMessage\\\": \\\"Remember to check for lint errors before proposing changes.\\\"\\r\\n          }\\r\\n        }\\r\\n      ],\\r\\n      \\\"terminationBehavior\\\": \\\"\\\"\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n\\r\\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array of objects, optional): Steps to inject.\\r\\n        Supported step types:\\r\\n        *   `{\\\"systemMessage\\\": {\\\"systemMessage\\\": \\\"...\\\", \\\"metadata\\\": {...}}}`\\r\\n            (persists in the conversation trajectory across turns; note that\\r\\n            `systemMessage` requires a nested object rather than a plain string).\\r\\n            To show markdown to the user that the model never reads, set\\r\\n            `metadata.user_markdown`: the UI renders it inline instead of a\\r\\n            collapsible \\\"System Message\\\" row, and the model sees only the\\r\\n            `systemMessage` text (leave it `\\\"\\\"` to send the model nothing).\\r\\n        *   `{\\\"userMessage\\\": \\\"...\\\"}` (plain string)\\r\\n        *   `{\\\"ephemeralMessage\\\": \\\"...\\\"}` *(deprecated — use `systemMessage`\\r\\n            instead; converted to a hidden system message)*\\r\\n        *   `{\\\"errorMessage\\\": {\\\"userMessage\\\": \\\"...\\\"}}` (shows an error message\\r\\n            to the user without sending it to the model. Marks the turn as\\r\\n            errored with a non-zero exit code in non-interactive `-p` mode, and\\r\\n            does not stop execution on its own unless paired with\\r\\n            `\\\"terminationBehavior\\\": \\\"terminate\\\"`).\\r\\n    *   **`terminationBehavior`** (string, optional):\\r\\n        *   `\\\"terminate\\\"`: Stops execution immediately before calling the model\\r\\n            (pair with an injected `errorMessage` to show a block message to the\\r\\n            user).\\r\\n        *   `\\\"\\\"` (or omitted): Proceeds to the model call (default).\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n### 5. `PostInvocation` Contract\\r\\n\\r\\nUse to inspect model outputs and potentially force continuation.\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Input (stdin)**: Same as `PreInvocation` input.\\r\\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"injectSteps\\\": [],\\r\\n      \\\"terminationBehavior\\\": \\\"force_continue\\\"\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n\\r\\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array, optional): Steps to inject (same supported\\r\\n        step types as `PreInvocation`).\\r\\n    *   **`terminationBehavior`** (string, optional):\\r\\n        *   `\\\"force_continue\\\"`: Forces the execution loop to continue.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"terminate\\\"`: Forces the loop to stop (pair with an injected\\r\\n            `errorMessage` to show a block message to the user).\\r\\n        *   `\\\"\\\"` (or omitted): Default behavior.\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n### 6. `Stop` Contract\\r\\n\\r\\nUse to prevent the agent from stopping if goals are not met.\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"executionNum\\\": 1,\\r\\n      \\\"terminationReason\\\": \\\"NO_TOOL_CALL\\\", // e.g., \\\"NO_TOOL_CALL\\\", \\\"MAX_INVOCATIONS\\\", \\\"ERROR\\\", \\\"USER_CANCELED\\\", \\\"TERMINAL_STEP_TYPE\\\"\\r\\n      \\\"error\\\": \\\"\\\", // Present if stopped due to error\\r\\n      \\\"fullyIdle\\\": true, // true if all background tasks are done\\r\\n      \\\"continuationActive\\\": true, // true after a Stop hook kept the agent going this turn (executionNum > 0)\\r\\n      ... (common fields)\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n    *   **`continuationActive`** (boolean): `true` while the agent is doing\\r\\n        work that a `Stop` hook asked for in this turn; `false` again once a\\r\\n        new user message starts a turn. Check it so a hook that asks the agent\\r\\n        to continue doesn't ask again in a loop.\\r\\n    *   **`terminationReason`** (string): The `ExecutorTerminationReason` enum\\r\\n        name with the `EXECUTOR_TERMINATION_REASON_` prefix stripped:\\r\\n        *   `\\\"NO_TOOL_CALL\\\"`: Normal turn completion (model finished without\\r\\n            calling a tool).\\r\\n        *   `\\\"MAX_INVOCATIONS\\\"`: Hit the maximum invocation limit.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"MAX_FORCED_INVOCATIONS\\\"`: Hit the forced-continuation limit.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"MAX_TOKEN_BUDGET_EXCEEDED\\\"`: Exceeded the token budget.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"ERROR\\\"`: Execution terminated due to an error.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"USER_CANCELED\\\"`: Canceled by the user.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"TERMINAL_STEP_TYPE\\\"`: Stopped on a terminal step type.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"TERMINAL_CUSTOM_HOOK\\\"`: Terminated by a custom `PreInvocation` or\\r\\n            `PostInvocation` hook.\\r\\n        *   `\\\"INJECTED_RESPONSE\\\"`: Terminated after an injected response.\\r\\n\\r\\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\\r\\n\\r\\n    ```json\\r\\n    {\\r\\n      \\\"decision\\\": \\\"continue\\\",\\r\\n      \\\"reason\\\": \\\"The tests are still running in the background. Please wait.\\\"\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    ```\\r\\n\\r\\n    *   **`decision`** (string, required): Set to `\\\"continue\\\"` to block the stop\\r\\n        and re-enter the loop. Any other value allows the agent to stop.\\r\\n    *   **`reason`** (string, optional): Injected as a system message if\\r\\n        continuing.\\r\\n\\r\\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\\r\\n\\r\\n## Current Limitations\\r\\n\\r\\n*   Only `type: \\\"command\\\"` is supported (no HTTP or prompt hooks yet).\\r\\n*   Hooks run synchronously and block the agent loop (no async ### AI Output (final_answer)

Verified local integrations:

- **Antigravity:** `.agents/hooks.json` is an actual Antigravity hook format. Bundled documentation confirms hooks run from the directory containing `hooks.json`, so `node scripts/log-ai-session.cjs` is correct here. Common payload fields are camelCase: `conversationId`, `workspacePaths`, `transcriptPath`, `modelName`. `Stop` runs when the execution loop terminates. `PostToolUse` expects `{}`, whereas `Stop` accepts a decision.
  - Evidence: `C:/Users/bacdo/.gemini/antigravity/builtin/skills/agy-customizations/docs/hooks.md`.
  - Both products have accessible transcripts: `~/.gemini/antigravity-ide/brain/*/.system_generated/logs/transcript.jsonl` (**32**) and `~/.gemini/antigravity/brain/*/.system_generated/logs/transcript.jsonl` (**2**).
  - Actual transcript records use `USER_INPUT` and `PLANNER_RESPONSE`; most planner records have thinking/tool calls without `content`. Sample: 23 user inputs, 294 planner records, 30 with visible content. Records themselves do not expose a separate workspace field; avoid importing other projects without a verified association.

- **Codex:** Installed CLI and real sessions for this repo exist under `~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl`.
  - `session_meta.payload.cwd` identifies the workspace; `id`, `parent_thread_id`, `forked_from_id`, `agent_path` distinguish sessions/subagents.
  - Actual chat events: `event_msg` with `payload.type=user_message` or `agent_message`; assistant events include `phase`. `response_item` messages also carry `role`, content blocks `input_text`/`output_text`, and `phase`, so naïvely reading both streams duplicates messages.
  - **Existing `~/.codex/config.toml` already sets `notify` to the Codex computer-use `turn-ended` executable. Preserve this integration.** Reading/watching native session files is an available alternative; adding a hook was not independently verified.

- **Claude Code:** Installed CLI. Existing repo conversations are in `~/.claude/projects/d--New-folder-workflow-automation/*.jsonl`.
  - Records have `cwd`, `sessionId`, `type=user|assistant`, `message.content`; assistant blocks include `text`, `thinking`, `tool_use`, with `message.stop_reason=end_turn|tool_use`.
  - Repo `.claude` has no settings/hook file. Global `.claude/settings.json` has no hooks.
  - Official locally cached Hookify plugin demonstrates `hooks.Stop`, `UserPromptSubmit`, `PostToolUse`, and snake_case `transcript_path` input. Evidence: `~/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/claude-plugins-official/plugins/hookify/hooks/hooks.json` and `core/rule_engine.py:209`.

- **Cursor:** Native readable transcripts exist at `~/.cursor/projects/<project>/agent-transcripts/*.txt`. Actual format contains `user:`, `assistant:`, `<user_query>`, `[Tool call]`, `[Tool result]`. Only another project exists locally; no Cursor transcript for this repo was found.

- **Gemini CLI:** Not verified installed. `~/.gemini/bin/agy.exe` exists and belongs to Antigravity.

No files changed. Some AppData application directories were denied by the sandbox; accessible native transcript sources above were verified without exposing message contents or credentials.

### AI Output (final_answer)

Structural discovery completed; no files changed or content exposed.

**Antigravity**

Evidence: `~/.gemini/antigravity/brain/29d11336-e865-445c-bcca-129ce3c3057e/.system_generated/logs/transcript_full.jsonl`.

Observed snapshot: `USER_INPUT:23`, `PLANNER_RESPONSE:294`, `GENERIC:295`, `SYSTEM_MESSAGE:11`, `CHECKPOINT:2`, `ERROR_MESSAGE:5`.

- Planner records expose `step_index`, `created_at`, `status`, optional `content`, and `tool_calls: Array<{name:string,args:object}>`. They also expose `thinking`, which should be excluded.
- Flattened `GENERIC` result records expose `step_index`, `source`, `type`, `status`, `created_at`, `content:string`, sometimes `error:string`. Their content is plain text, not serialized JSON.
- Neither flattened planner calls nor generic results expose a call ID. Preserve actual step indexes and order; avoid claiming precise correlation for parallel calls.
- **Use `transcript_full.jsonl`:** it preserves native argument numbers/booleans; `transcript.jsonl` stringifies these and truncates some fields.
- **Even full transcripts can omit tool output.** Among 165 generic records with `steps/<step_index>/output.txt`, five transcript contents did not contain the complete sidecar. Example: step 4 transcript content 10,316 bytes versus sidecar 16,127 bytes. Full transcript had no `truncated_fields` flags. Include sidecar output separately.
- Async command output exists in `tasks/task-<result-step_index>.log`; seven observed task files corresponded to `GENERIC` steps immediately following `run_command` planners. Some files were empty.
- No standalone root-session workspace metadata found. Subagent manifests expose `{conversationId,subagentDescriptor,state,spawnStepIndex,workspaceUris:string[]}` under `.system_generated/subagents/*.json`. Global `.gemini/config/projects/*.json` exposes `projectResources.resources[].folderUri`, but does **not** connect conversations to projects. Hook-context bindings are safer than inspecting prompt text for repo paths.

**Codex**

Evidence: `~/.codex/sessions/2026/11/22/rollout-2026-11-22T19-37-21-01a20342-d67c-76d3-9f99-2ad2ef66b643.jsonl`.

Native `response_item.payload` schemas:

- `function_call`: `{type,id,status,name,namespace?,arguments:string,call_id}`.
- `custom_tool_call`: `{type,id,status,name,input:string,call_id}`.
- Both output types: `{type,id,call_id,output:string | Array<{type,text}>}`.
- Inputs and outputs also contain `internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough.turn_id`; outputs may include `create_time`.
- Ten calls and ten outputs matched by `call_id` in the later snapshot. Observed array-output block type: `input_text`.
- Native logs expose submitted tool arguments and returned output. They cannot restore output the tool itself truncated or decrypt encrypted reasoning.

**Claude Code**

Evidence: `~/.claude/projects/d--New-folder-workflow-automation/94632c90-0d10-4462-937a-f4597d26f6ee.jsonl`.

- Assistant blocks: `{type:"tool_use",id,name,input:object,caller?}`.
- User blocks: `{type:"tool_result",tool_use_id,content:string|array,is_error?}`.
- Actual array results included image blocks `{type,source:{type,data,media_type}}` and tool-reference blocks `{type,tool_name}`.
- Rows may additionally expose structured `toolUseResult`, including stdout/stderr, interruption flags, file contents, or patches.
- Filter top-level rows to `user` and `assistant`: `api-request-blob` repeats tool blocks and causes duplicates.
- With that filter, all 447 tool uses matched 447 results by `tool_use.id === tool_result.tool_use_id`. Thinking blocks should be excluded.
